# Threat model: the invariant register

#442 (merged 2026-10-24) removed the mobile shell's gateway code (the Expo plugin, the `everyapp://` origin, the Access mobile handoff); the five invariants that described it are gone from this register.

This is the complete register behind [docs/threat-model.md](threat-model.md): every invariant each trust boundary holds, with the code that enforces it (`path:line` as of 2026-10-23, the ToolBus, MCP and audit rows re-read on 2026-10-24 for the compact surface; line numbers drift, symbol names less) and the tests that pin it (`file :: test name`, verbatim; at most three of each are listed). The threat model quotes the ones to try first; this file has all of them. "Partial", "unpinned" and "NOT ENFORCED" are marked in the last column.

Produced from a read of the code and a run of every cited test on 2026-10-23 (commit `c0f916ad`); regenerate by hand when a boundary changes. The ids are stable; cite them in issues and reports.


## B1. Browser → app host: the perimeter

34 invariants. The perimeter is handleGatewayRequest (packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts) as wired by apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts: it decides the host class, resolves and status-gates the registry row, refuses the internal tools contract and OAuth metadata paths, guards TanStack server functions, refuses a request carrying the session cookie twice, applies CSRF (and the WebSocket Origin rule) to cookie-carrying state changes, classifies the path public-or-private with fail-closed canonicalization, authenticates (Better Auth cookie, per-host session or personal access token) and authorizes (live membership, active organization, user_app_access or shared-app policy), then strips every inbound trust header and injects either a fresh RS256 identity JWT (aud = Worker name, 120 s, kid everyapp-identity) or a signed public marker before reaching the app through the single getAppFetcher seam. On the way out it strips Domain from app cookies, enforces a CSP floor on HTML and stamps HSTS/nosniff/frame denial on every non-101 response; third-party hosts additionally lose credentialed CORS and reserved __Host-everyapp_* cookies. It protects the session cookie from ever reaching app code, apps from forged identity or public markers, members from cross-site/sibling-app request forgery, one organization's Worker from another's tokens, and app Workers from being reached except through the gateway's bindings. Its weakest points are a first-party WebSocket 101 that passes through with Set-Cookie Domain intact, the 30-second per-isolate caches that bound revocation latency, and the duplicate-cookie refusal doubling as a lockout primitive.

| perimeter.public-auth-optional | Public is auth-optional, not auth-stripped: a signed-in member with access who passes CSRF gets the identity JWT on a public route, a non-member gets the marker, a non-member on a private route gets 403; an authenticator failure is swallowed only on public routes (anonymous) and rethrown on private ones (502 gateway_error from server.ts). | Fail-open on a private route when the auth backend errors; members being served logged-out pages on overlapping public/private surfaces. | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:377-400`; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:471-493`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts:405-418` | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: (d2) a public route with a signed-in member injects identity, not the marker (auth-optional, not auth-stripped); `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: (d4) a private route with a signed-in NON-member is still 403; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: (g) 403 when the user has no access to the app |
| perimeter.app-access-policy | Access to an organization's own app requires a live, non-banned membership in the app's organization (a browser session acting under another of the person's organizations is re-resolved as that membership; a token acts only in its own organization), the organization active, and a user_app_access row; a shared app requires platformPolicy.sharedApps, a live member of any active organization, acting under the session's organization; an app-bound token opens only its app; tokens holding `deploy` or any `provider:` scope open no app. | Members of another organization, removed/deactivated members, suspended organizations, deploy keys, or an app-bound token reaching an app they were not granted; a JWT carrying claims for an organization the person does not act in. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:55-76` (resolveLiveOrgRole); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:79-92` (boundToAnotherApp); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:114-122` (role, provider:, deploy scope) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: opens no app with a deploy key, an owner's included; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: rejects the PAT of a deactivated (banned) user; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: returns null when the token user is no longer a member of the token org |
| perimeter.mcp-only-token-scope | A credential whose scopes are all `mcp:*` is served at exactly /mcp, and only where the host's manifest has `mcp` and MCP is served there (the ToolBus for `by: "gateway"`, the app's own server for `by: "app"`); it answers 403 token_scope on every other app route (public ones and /mcp/ or /MCP included), on every route of a host without `mcp` (/mcp included) and at a /mcp a public route covers on a `by: "gateway"` host; tokens holding `*` or any non-MCP scope are unaffected. | An mcp:read token (read-only by contract) calling the app's own write routes, which do not check scopes. | `packages/perimeter/src/session.ts:32-37` (isMcpOnlyCredential); `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts` (the `mcpServedHere` check in handleGatewayRequest) | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: refuse every app route but /mcp with 403 token_scope; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: open nothing on a host whose manifest serves no MCP, /mcp included; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: open nothing at a /mcp a public route covers, even where the manifest opts in; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: let them through at /mcp, to the ToolBus or to the app's own server; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: leave full-access and API-scoped tokens on every route |

| perimeter.unauthenticated-private-response | An unauthenticated private request gets a 302 to `${GATEWAY_URL}/sign-in?return_to=<url>` (first-party) or the per-host handoff (third-party) only for an HTML navigation; everything else gets 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="every-app"` (plus an invalid_token description pointing at the gateway's /mcp when a bearer was presented), all with security headers. | Leaking app content to anonymous callers; redirecting API/MCP clients into HTML; advertising an authorization server on app hosts. | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:169-190` (bearerChallenge, bearerJson); `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:471-490`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts:402` (loginUrl) | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: (c) returns 401 when unauthenticated on a private route; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: stamps security headers on the login redirect; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: invalid PAT bearer returns bearer 401 JSON without login redirect |

Not this boundary's job: Sibling apps share the session cookie's delivery domain: a cookie-carrying GET fired from another app's page (or an XSS in it) is CSRF-exempt and gets a fresh identity for the target app; apps must keep GETs side-effect-free (an accepted risk).; App code trusting anything other than the verified identity JWT / public marker (forwarding headers, its own cookies), and apps re-exposed outside the gateway (workers.dev, routes): deploy-time policy, not a runtime check.; Replay of a captured identity JWT within its 120 s lifetime (jti is minted but not checked) and key rotation: the SDK verification boundary.; Rate limiting, metering, bot protection or cost controls on app hosts' public routes and 401 paths; the control plane's credential rate limits do not apply to app hosts.; Business-level authentication of public routes (webhook signatures, API keys): the gateway forwards app-owned Authorization blind.; Containing an app's own XSS beyond the CSP floor (frame-ancestors/base-uri/object-src) and X-Frame-Options; app JS writing parent-domain cookies via document.cookie.; The control plane's own guards (its CSRF rule, the sign-in page's handling of `return_to`, Better Auth endpoints), the gateway's own /mcp and OAuth server, and the ToolBus's tool-call semantics once /mcp is intercepted: separate boundaries.; Egress from service-binding (self-hosted) apps, which can fetch anywhere; dispatch-tier egress is the Outbound worker's boundary.; Operator authority through Cloudflare (D1 writes to the registry, redeploying the gateway, reading JWT_PRIVATE_KEY): ADR-0003 trusts anyone with the account.; Cloudflare Access outer-ring semantics (who Access admits, service tokens) beyond the perimeter stripping cf-access-* headers; DNS, certificates and the Workers route/Custom Domain that bring an app host to this Worker..



## B2. Browser → the Gateway's own host: the control plane

46 invariants. The control plane is the Gateway Worker's own host: Better Auth sessions (one cookie spanning the app subdomains), the one-time setup claim that makes the first owner, invitation links, the per-organization role model (owner/admin/member) behind TanStack server functions, and the platform policy that decides, from GATEWAY_DEPLOYMENT_MODE, what a gateway may do at all. It protects the account database (who can sign in, as whom, in which organization), the owner/admin roles that gate every app grant and member action, the secrets in links (setup, invitation, reset tokens, stored as SHA-256 only), and the gateway's outbound-mail reputation. Auth configuration itself (Access, password sign-in, email, deployment mode) lives only in Worker secrets: a stolen admin session can act on members of its organization within the role rules but cannot change how anyone signs in, touch an owner, deploy, or read secrets. Every invariant below was verified by reading the code and re-running the pinning test files, all of which pass.

| setup.one-time-link | A claim succeeds only with an est_ token whose SHA-256 equals, in constant time, the hash of an unconsumed, unexpired claim row; the row is consumed by a guarded UPDATE (consumed_at IS NULL) as the claim's last step, so a link makes at most one owner. | Guessing or replaying the setup link to become owner, and timing attacks on the hash comparison. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.ts:79-88` (findClaimLink), 111-115, 213-215; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/OwnerSetupTokenRepository.ts:6-17` (findActiveClaims), 23-36 (consume); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/setup-token.ts:10-28` (hashSetupToken, digestsEqual) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: rejects a wrong token with 403 and creates nothing; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: rejects a used or expired link with 403 (only live links are matched); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: rolls back if the link was consumed by another claim |

| setup.one-claim-at-a-time-rollback | Only one claim runs at a time (owner_bootstrap singleton INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING); a concurrent claim gets 503 CLAIM_IN_PROGRESS; a failed claim deletes the user and organization it made and releases the bootstrap; a claim killed mid-way goes stale after 120 s and the next claim clears it and its unaffiliated account. | Two claims racing to two owners; a half-finished claim leaving an ownerless gateway, or an orphan account with the claimant's email. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/organization/owner-membership.ts:8`,21-62; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.ts:120-132`,223-233; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/UserRepository.ts:28-46` (deleteUnaffiliatedByEmail) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: lets only one of two concurrent claims through; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: asks the retry of a claim killed part-way to wait, not to sign in; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/SetupService.test.ts` :: recovers from a claim that died part-way and left a stale bootstrap |

| roles.owner-immutable-no-self-admin-needs-owner | An organization always keeps at least one owner. Owners are peers: an owner can make a member or an admin an owner, and change the role of, deactivate or delete another owner, but never themselves and never the last owner (LAST_OWNER; the owner count is read from the database at the time, not from the caller's context, and the write itself enforces it: the demotion, ban or removal is one conditional UPDATE or DELETE that refuses the organization's last owner who can act, so two owners acting on each other at once can't both succeed and the second answers LAST_OWNER); an admin manages members only, never an admin or an owner (OWNER_REQUIRED); only an owner can hand out the owner role; invitations carry member or admin only; a password reset for an owner is refused whoever asks, another owner included (CANNOT_MANAGE_OWNER). | An admin demoting or locking out an owner or a peer admin; Not this boundary's job: Cloudflare Access sign-in, the outer ring, OIDC linking and Access accepting pending invitations for a verified email without the link secret (membership.ts:163-200): the Access lane; the control plane only relies on policy.accessSignIn being false on hosted; The perimeter on app hosts (identity JWT minting, app-host CSRF, header stripping, third-party per-host sessions) beyond the shared session cookie and ban/role checks cited here; Personal access tokens and the gateway /mcp OAuth server (UserPatService, mcp-auth): their scopes and revocation on removal/deactivation are cited, their issuance is not; The deploy API, browser install routes (server/deploy/install.ts authenticates with the same session/role/ban/suspension checks) and the CLI's direct D1 writes other than the setup link (ADR-0003); App catalog management (AppService), shared-app registration rules and the apps lane's shared-apps.sqlite.test.ts; Compromise of the operator's Cloudflare account or Worker secrets (BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, GATEWAY_URL, mode): the root of trust on self-host per ADR-0001; the control plane cannot defend against whoever can rewrite D1 or secrets; Enumeration of whether an email has an account through timing or wording of Better Auth's sign-in/reset responses (Better Auth's uniform answers are relied on, not re-tested); Browser-side XSS in the control-plane UI (CSP floor is stamped; React rendering is assumed safe) and phishing of the setup or invitation links themselves; Denial of service against D1 or the Worker beyond credential rate limits and mail budgets.



## B3. Gateway → app Worker: the identity contract

39 invariants. This boundary is what lets an app Worker believe who is calling it. The Gateway (perimeter, ToolBus, catalog sync) mints three kinds of short-lived RS256 JWTs for one Worker name (aud): user identities (120 s; chan web/api/mcp, actor, scopes, optional tc), public-route markers, and one-purpose system tokens (60 s, tools.list). The SDK's everyApp() verifies them fail-closed (alg/kid pinned, env key set, aud = EVERYAPP_AUDIENCE, iss = EVERYAPP_IDENTITY_ISSUER, typ separation) before any app code runs, and answers the reserved /__everyapp/tools/* contract itself. The ToolBus turns an authenticated MCP caller into a principal (read/write access from the credential), hides destructive tools, bounds arguments and app answers, mints one call-bound identity per tools/call and audits a keyed digest. The AppGateway entrypoint decides which organization an app's provider call counts against from the binding's upload-time props, a forwarded identity, or a shared app's x-everyapp-org, and the SDK's createGatewayFetch/integrationFetch send only those two forms of attribution. The dev keypair is public; it is honoured only under EVERYAPP_DEV, with the dev issuer, which a production build never sets.

Not this boundary's job: How the perimeter authenticates sessions, PATs, OAuth tokens and Cloudflare Access assertions, and the CSRF, cookie-tossing and Origin rules on app hosts and the gateway's /mcp (perimeter and MCP-auth boundaries); this map only relies on the perimeter stripping x-everyapp-* and 404ing /__everyapp/tools/*; Whether the Gateway's JWT_PRIVATE_KEY is kept secret and how it is provisioned, rotated or backed up (operator authority / CLI boundary); every invariant here assumes only the gateway can sign; The registry rows themselves (who may register an app, change its scope, status or worker_name) and the hosted deployer that writes binding props for dispatch-tier apps (control plane / deploy boundaries); Authorization inside an app: the SDK delivers a verified user with orgRole and scopes, but what a member, viewer or read-only token may do with an app's data is the app's own code; Helper Workers verify no identities; apps hand them ids as arguments (TODO: 'Helper Workers verify no identities'); Anything on the local dev ports: the dev keypair is public by design, everyapp dev listens on 127.0.0.1, and anyone who can reach those ports can mint a dev identity (packages/sdk/src/dev/keypair.ts:1-10); Provider-side behaviour (what OpenAI, DataForSEO, LinkedIn do with a forwarded request) and the capability allow-lists per route (capabilities boundary); Denial of service against the gateway's /mcp beyond the per-request bounds listed (body, depth, values, timeouts, 8 listen streams per credential per isolate); there is no global rate limit; Prompt-injection through tool descriptions, instructions or results: the ToolBus bounds their size but passes their content to the AI client as the app wrote it.



## B4. App Worker → the Gateway: the AppGateway binding, AI and capabilities

53 invariants. The gateway holds every provider credential (OpenAI, OpenRouter, Workers AI, DataForSEO, Context.dev keys; the keys of the API providers an operator registered on this gateway, rows of its providers table; 18 OAuth client secrets; the Email Sending/Resend transport and EMAIL_FROM) and lends their effect, never the secret, to app Workers that reach it through the EVERY_APP_GATEWAY binding. The boundary must (1) keep the credential out of every request the app can see and every log; (2) confine calls to inference or token endpoints on the provider's own https hosts so one app cannot read, list or delete what another organization did under the shared account; (3) attribute every call to an organization (the binding's upload-time props for an organization's own app; a verified RS256 identity or a checked x-everyapp-org for a shared app) and refuse calls that name another organization; (4) let apps send mail only as the gateway's sender or an installation's allowlisted From, within daily budgets, with reserved headers refused and logs that never carry subjects, bodies or addresses; (5) hand an app only its own organization's live members and refuse agents. What it does not do: bound an organization's spend unless an owner or admin capped the app (`cap.per-organization-limits`: counted always, refused only at an opt-in cap), or budget app mail per organization.

<!-- generated from capabilities -->

| Id | Invariant | Stops | Enforced by | Pinned by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ai.key-never-reaches-app | The upstream AI request carries only the gateway's Authorization: Bearer <key> and none of the app's credentials, account selectors (x-api-key, openai-organization, openai-project), identity/public/org headers (x-everyapp-*), x-user-*, cookie, AI Gateway (cf-aig-*), hop-by-hop or forwarding headers; the key appears in no response or log. | An app steering the shared key onto its own OpenAI organization/project or AI Gateway cache, smuggling identities, or exfiltrating the key through a failed-request log. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:13-42` (STRIPPED_PROXY_HEADERS, STRIPPED_PROXY_HEADER_PREFIXES); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:220-222` (stripProxyHeaders; requestHeaders.set authorization); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:439-448` (stripProxyHeaders) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: forwards request to OpenAI with gateway-managed key and strips internal headers; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: logs an upstream failure without the request it carried; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: ignores app-supplied Authorization and injects the gateway provider key |
| ai.inference-only-by-default | Without both the operator's AI_PROXY_EXTRA_ROUTES and the app manifest's ai.routes covering the path, only POST to the provider's exact inferenceRoutes is forwarded; GET on an inference path, files, batches, fine-tunes, assistants and stored responses are refused 403 ai_route_not_allowed before any upstream call. | An app using the shared key to list, read or delete another organization's files, batches, fine-tunes or stored completions, or to start expensive jobs. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:78-88` (openai inferenceRoutes); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:312-327` (isAllowedRoute); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:204-218` (ai_route_not_allowed) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: rejects non-inference endpoints (files, batches, stored responses); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: needs the app's opt-in too, whatever the operator allows; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: ignores a manifest's opt-in the operator has not allowed |
| ai.extra-routes-openai-only | AI_PROXY_EXTRA_ROUTES and ai.routes widen OpenAI only; OpenRouter and Workers AI serve their inference routes and nothing else whatever the operator or manifest says, so OpenRouter's /api/v1/credits and /api/v1/keys and Workers AI's /v1/run, /run/<model>, /v1/models, /finetunes stay closed. | An app reading the operator's OpenRouter credit balance or API keys, or running Workers AI's native/finetune endpoints, under a widening meant for OpenAI. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:97-104` (openrouter inferenceRoutes, extraRoutes: false); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:117-123` (workers-ai inferenceRoutes, extraRoutes: false); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:322-326` (provider.extraRoutes &&) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: allows inference only, whatever the operator allows for OpenAI; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: serves the OpenAI-compatible inference routes only |
| ai.store-false | On OpenAI's /v1/responses and /v1/chat/completions the gateway rewrites the JSON body's store to false, so no app's completions are retained account-wide where another app could read them. | One app's prompts and outputs being stored under the shared OpenAI account and retrieved by another app via stored-response endpoints. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:89` (unstoredRoutes); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:380-382` (body.store = false) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: forces store:false on responses and chat completions; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: leaves other inference bodies as they are |
| ai.model-allowlist | When the manifest sets ai.models, an inference request whose body (JSON model field or multipart model part) names a model outside the list, or none, is refused 403 model_not_allowed before any upstream call; opted-in non-inference routes are not model-checked. | An app exceeding the model tier its deployer declared (cost control), on OpenAI, OpenRouter and Workers AI alike. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:234-246` (model_not_allowed); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:347-360` (multipart model); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:387` (JSON model) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: enforces the manifest's model allowlist; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: applies the manifest's model allowlist; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: enforces the manifest's ai.models on @cf model ids |
| ai.path-confinement | The app's path after /api/ai/<provider> is joined onto the provider's base URL (Workers AI's /client/v4/accounts/<id>/ai kept) and can never replace or leave it: '//' and backslashes are refused 400, dot segments are resolved by the URL parser before routing, an encoded slash matches no allowed route, and the query string is passed as-is. | SSRF or key exfiltration by steering the upstream to another host, or reaching another account's or another API's path on api.cloudflare.com with the Workers AI token. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:396-421` (buildUpstreamUrl); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:427-437` (isSafeUpstreamPath); `packages/sdk/src/internal/aiProviders.ts:62-73` (joinProviderUrl) | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: rejects proxy paths that attempt to override upstream host; `packages/sdk/src/internal/aiProviders.test.ts` :: refuses a path that would leave the base's path; `packages/sdk/src/internal/aiProviders.test.ts` :: joins the path onto the base's own path and sets the query |
| ai.provider-grant | An AI call reaches the proxy only for a provider the app's registry-row manifest lists in providers; otherwise 403 provider_not_allowed, before any key-existence check. | An app using a provider its deployer never declared, or probing which provider keys the operator holds. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:202-209` (provider_not_allowed) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: denies a provider that is not allowlisted; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: denies proxy access when providers is absent; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: refuses provider_not_allowed without the grant, in OpenAI's error shape |
| ai.not-configured-fails-closed | A provider whose key (or, for Workers AI, account id) is unset or blank answers 503 ai_provider_not_configured naming the settings, with no upstream call; another provider's key never substitutes. | Calls going out with an empty bearer or the wrong provider's key; misconfiguration silently working. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:184-196` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: returns 503 when provider secret is not configured; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: refuses 503 ai_provider_not_configured, naming both settings, while either is unset; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: returns 404 for unknown provider |
| ai.logs-redacted | AI logs (ai.proxy.usage, ai.proxy.upstream_failed) carry provider, organizationId, appId, route, model, status, x-request-id and token usage read off the stream tail, never the prompt, the output or the key. | Prompt/output or key leakage through Workers Logs. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:281-295` (logUsage, tapUsage); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:261-277` (message only on failure); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-usage.ts:22-34` (extractUsage), :40-59 (tapUsage) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: logs model, token usage and request id, never the prompt; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: logs an upstream failure without the request it carried; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-usage.test.ts` :: passes the body through and reports usage at the end |
| ai.refusal-shape | Every refusal the AI proxy or the entrypoint makes on /api/ai/* carries x-everyapp-error and OpenAI's error body; the provider's own errors pass through without the header. | An app mistaking a provider error for a gateway refusal or vice versa (and so mis-handling key problems); not an attack but a correctness guarantee the SDK's gatewayRefusal relies on. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.ts:465-474` (aiRefusal); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:513-517` (errorResponse on /api/ai/) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: carry x-everyapp-error and OpenAI's error shape, code and message; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/ai-proxy.test.ts` :: are not added to the provider's own errors |
| cap.grant-required | A capability call is forwarded only when the calling app's registry-row manifest lists that capability (api_key: any entry; email and oauth_client: access 'write'; members: access 'read'); otherwise 403 capability_not_requested before the key or client is consulted. | An app using a provider, client or service its deployer never declared, and learning whether the operator holds it. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:164-170`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/email.ts:89-92`, :150-156; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.ts:34-36`, :116-122 | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: refuses a capability the app's registry row doesn't list (%s); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/oauth-client.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses an app whose manifest doesn't, and tells it nothing; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.test.ts` :: refuses an app that doesn't list the client, or lists it for reading |
| cap.host-allowlist-https | The upstream of an api_key call is https://<host><path> only for a host in the provider entry's hosts list, or the one its hostSetting names (a bare host or https origin with nothing else, lowercased), matched exactly before any URL is built; a path starting '//' or containing a backslash, or a parse that moves the host, is refused 400 invalid_integration_route; an AI provider's id is never routed here (400) however it is spelled, so its key never leaves the AI proxy's inference allowlist; the SDK refuses non-https and, for a catalog entry, foreign hosts before the call leaves the app, and sends an id outside the catalog over the binding for the gateway to decide. | Sending the provider key to an attacker's host (SSRF/key exfiltration) via the host segment, a protocol-relative path, a parser quirk, a host setting with a path, or an AI key reaching the account endpoints on its own host. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/capabilities.ts` (parseIntegrationProxyPath, httpsUpstream, apiKeyHosts, hostSettingValue); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts` (the route parse with env and lookup) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: refuses %s before anything leaves the gateway; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/provider-rules.test.ts` :: reaches the host a provider's host setting names, and no other; :: never routes an AI provider or an unknown id through the capability proxy; `packages/sdk/src/internal/capabilities.test.ts` :: takes the host a provider's hostSetting names, when it is one host |
| cap.account-wide-paths-refused | DataForSEO paths that list or describe the whole account (tasks_ready, tasks_fixed, id_list, errors, appendix/user_data, appendix/webhook_resend) are refused 403 integration_path_not_allowed however spelled: percent-encoded once or twice, another case, a suffix on the segment (',', ';x', '.json', '%20', '%00'), doubled or backslash separators, a trailing segment, a dot segment revealed only by decoding, non-ASCII, or a path that will not decode. | One organization's app enumerating every other organization's DataForSEO task ids (then fetching their results by id), the account balance, or re-sending webhooks. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/capabilities.ts:153-159` (accountWidePaths); `packages/sdk/src/internal/capabilities.ts:1068-1092` (isAccountWidePath), :1095-1108 (decodePath); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:171-177` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: refuses DataForSEO's account-wide %s, which would show one app another's tasks; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: still serves a task's own results by id, and posting tasks; `packages/sdk/src/internal/capabilities.test.ts` :: refuses %s |
| cap.read-methods-only | An api_key call is forwarded with a method in the provider entry's readMethods on any grant (GET, POST for DataForSEO and Context.dev), or with one of its writeMethods only when the entry offers write access, the app's stored manifest entry says access: 'write', and a calling agent's connection carries mcp:write (403 integration_read_only otherwise); every other method answers 405 integration_method_not_allowed. | cap.credentials-stripped-no-redirect | The upstream api_key request carries the gateway's Authorization and none of the app's Authorization, x-api-key, cookie, identity/public/org/x-everyapp-*, x-user-*, hop-by-hop or forwarding headers, and is made with redirect: 'manual' so the key never follows a 3xx to another host. | An app substituting its own credential, smuggling identities to the provider, or a redirecting provider leaking the key to a third party. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/forward.ts:4-29` (STRIPPED_HEADERS, prefix), :35-42 (forwardableHeaders); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:199-210` (authorization set; redirect: manual) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: forwards a call with the gateway's key and nothing the app sent to authenticate |
| cap.response-cookies-dropped | A provider's response reaches the app with its status, body and headers minus set-cookie, and without x-everyapp-error, so provider errors are distinguishable from gateway refusals. | Provider session cookies accumulating in or being replayed by apps; misattribution of errors. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:241-242`, :256-260; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.ts:200-206` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: passes the provider's own errors through without the gateway's error header; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.test.ts` :: drops the provider's cookies and passes the rest of its answer through |
| cap.key-existence-hidden | Only an app that lists a capability learns whether the gateway holds its key, client or sender: GET /api/integrations/<id> answers configured:false (and no clientId/sender/provider) for an app that does not list it, and calls refuse capability_not_requested before integration_not_configured. | Reconnaissance of the operator's provider inventory by any deployed app. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:100-127` (status), :164-196 (order of refusals); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/email.ts:108-127`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.ts:44-65` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: reports whether calls would go through, without calling the provider; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: says who sets the key when the gateway has none; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.test.ts` :: tells an app that doesn't list the client nothing, its id included |
| cap.logs-redacted | integration.proxy.call logs capability, organizationId, appId, worker, orgSource, host, path, method, status, attribution/userId and (DataForSEO) the response's top-level cost; upstream failures log the error message (api_key) or only the error name (oauth_client); never a key, a body, a query string, a code or a token. | Credential, code or token leakage through logs, including a fetch error message that quotes a URL carrying the client secret. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:212-220`, :226-233, :243-248, :267-294 (tapCost); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.ts:171-180`, :187-190, :198 | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: logs the call's cost from the response, never its body or key; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.test.ts` :: answers an unreachable provider with a refusal that never echoes the key; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/capabilities/oauthClient.test.ts` :: logs the call and its grant type, never the client, the code or the tokens |
| providers.query-key-never-echoed | For a provider whose auth kind is query, the gateway's key replaces every copy of the parameter the app sent (URLSearchParams.set), a 3xx answer is never relayed (502 integration_upstream_redirected, the body cancelled), location, content-location and refresh are deleted from every answer, and an upstream failure logs the error's name only, never a message that could quote the URL. | A provider's redirect or URL-naming header handing the key back to the app, or a failed-request log carrying the URL the key travels in. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts` (the query branches: the parameter set, the 3xx refusal, the header deletes, the name-only log) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/provider-rules.test.ts` :: puts a query key on the URL in place of the app's, relays no redirect and no URL-naming header, and logs no message on failure |

| providers.row-secret-is-its-own | Every Gateway setting a registered provider's row names (its auth secret, a Basic username held as a setting, its hostSetting) matches ^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,63}$, starts with the row's id upper-cased with '-' as '_' (STRIPE_ for stripe), and is nobody else's: never an install-owned setting (JWT_PRIVATE_KEY, BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN, ...), a dev-only one, a reserved app-secret name (EVERYAPP_*, EVERY_APP_*, APP__*), a settable Gateway setting (every catalog key and client, MCP_*, CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_*, EMAIL_*) or an earlier row's; a row that breaks it is refused PROVIDER_SECRET_TAKEN and skipped by the gateway on every read (logged provider.row_invalid), the earlier row keeping its setting. | A row pointing the gateway's signing key, a catalog provider's key or another row's key at a host the row names, where one app call would send it. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.ts` (rowSecretProblem, checkProviderRow, providerRegistry's rowSettings); `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerSpec.ts` (PROVIDER_SECRET_NAME) | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.test.ts` :: refuses a row naming %s; :: gives a second row naming the first's setting to the first; :: refuses the prefix rule's other cases through rowSecretProblem (partial: the refusal of a name a registered app lists under secrets, and the deploy's refusal of an app secret a row holds, are the CLI's half of PR 2b and are not pinned here) |
| providers.value-bound-to-hosts | The gateway reads a registered provider's settings only while the row's bound_to equals providerBindingKey of the row it has now (its hosts, hostSetting and auth as one canonical string), which everyapp secret set records when it sets the key; a row never bound, replaced, removed and added again, or edited by hand answers 503 integration_not_configured ('the key was set for other hosts') with no upstream call, its status configured: false, and its settings read as unset in every app's setup report. | Whoever can rewrite rows moving a key the operator set to another host (replacing the row, or removing it and adding it again under the same setting name, which outlives the row). | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.ts` (providerBindingKey; the registry's bound and unboundSettings); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts` (the lookup.bound check; the status's configured); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-setup.ts` (appSettingsReport with unboundSettings) | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerBinding.test.ts` :: reports the catalog bound, a row bound only while its key matches, and an unknown id unbound; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/providers/providers.test.ts` :: reads the key for a row bound to the hosts it has, and not after the hosts change; :: answers an unbound row's status as not configured, and its app as needing setup |
| providers.row-hosts-public-https | Each host a registered provider's row names is a bare lowercase DNS name with a dot (no scheme, port, userinfo, path or wildcard) that is not an IPv4 literal, localhost, or under .localhost, .local, .internal or .arpa, and is neither the gateway's own host (GATEWAY_URL's) nor under APP_BASE_DOMAIN or THIRD_PARTY_DOMAIN; a row naming one is refused PROVIDER_HOST_REFUSED and dropped on every read, logged provider.row_invalid with the id and the reason once per isolate, never the row's spec; calls go to https://<host> only, with redirect: 'manual'. | A row sending a key into the gateway's own control plane, an app host, a third-party app host or an internal address. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerSpec.ts` (PROVIDER_HOST); `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.ts` (publicHostProblem, checkProviderRow); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/providers/index.ts` (refusedGatewayHost, registryFromRows) | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.test.ts` :: refuses hosts that aren't public, and the ones this gateway serves; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/providers/providers.test.ts` :: refuses its own host and anything under its app and third-party domains; :: drops a row on such a host, logging the id and the reason once (partial: a hostSetting's value is held to the shape rule when read, hostSettingValue, not yet to the public-host and gateway-host rules) |
| providers.builtin-precedence | A row never takes a catalog id or everyapp (PROVIDER_BUILTIN); the registry resolves the catalog before the rows, so nothing a row says changes where a catalog provider's key goes; a row a later catalog entry collides with (its id or its settings) is skipped and logged, never served; a second row with an id already accepted is skipped. | A row shadowing DataForSEO, or a catalog entry added later, to send its key elsewhere. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.ts` (checkProviderRow: RESERVED_IDS, CAPABILITY_IDS; providerRegistry: the catalog first) | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.test.ts` :: never lets a row shadow the catalog or take the reserved id |
| providers.spec-names-no-values | A provider spec names Gateway settings and has no field that holds a value: the parser refuses every unknown field (at the top level and in auth, secrets[], smoke, cost and rate) and control characters in any text, auth.secret, auth.user.secret and hostSetting must be setting names, auth.header a header name that isn't one the gateway sets or strips (host, cookie, content-length, transfer-encoding, x-everyapp-*, ...) and auth.scheme a token; a stored row is parsed again on every read, and a refusal is logged by the parser's message, never the row's text. | A key pasted into a spec field (and so into D1 and every listing of it), or a header that smuggles the key into one the proxy rewrites. | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerSpec.ts` (parseProviderSpec: KNOWN_FIELDS, UNPRINTABLE, secretName, RESERVED_HEADERS, SCHEME); `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.ts` (resolveProviderRow) | `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerSpec.test.ts` :: names every problem at once, with its path; :: makes basic say where the key goes, and refuses a header the gateway owns; `packages/sdk/src/internal/providerRegistry.test.ts` :: drops a row whose spec doesn't parse or doesn't match its id, naming why |

| attr.caller-identity | Only a registered, active app whose binding props (organizationId, appId, workerName from ctx.props) exactly match its registry row reaches any proxy; malformed props are 401 missing_caller_identity, unknown apps 403 caller_not_registered, inactive 403 caller_not_active, mismatches 403 caller_identity_mismatch, and a shared app on a gateway without shared apps is refused. | A Worker impersonating another app or organization, or a disabled app continuing to spend. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.ts:228-241` (AppGateway passes this.ctx.props); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:112-160`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:257-277` (parseCallerProps) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: accepts valid props that match an active registry row; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: rejects missing or malformed props with a structured 401; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: rejects a caller from the wrong organization |
| attr.org-app-own-organization | An organization's own app's call is attributed to its props organization: a forwarded identity names the user only if it verifies for the Worker (aud = workerName, iss = GATEWAY_URL) and the same organization, otherwise the call is the app's; an x-everyapp-org naming another organization is refused 403 caller_org_mismatch and the organization directory is never consulted. | An org-scoped app charging or attributing calls to another organization, or a user of another organization (or a token minted for another Worker) being named on this app's calls. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:363-373`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:473-486` (verifyForwardedIdentity: audience workerName, issuer GATEWAY_URL); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.ts:488-511` (resolveAttribution) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: refuses an x-everyapp-org naming another organization, and never consults the directory; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: never attributes an identity minted for another Worker, even of the same app id; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: falls back to app attribution when a forwarded identity is invalid |

| attr.status-needs-no-organization | GET /api/integrations/<id> (status) is answered without attribution: it calls no provider and names no organization's data. | Nothing to stop; the invariant bounds what the unattributed path can reveal (configuration flags only, and only to apps that list the capability). | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/integration-proxy.ts:100-127` (returns before admit) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/app-gateway-entrypoint.test.ts` :: answers a capability's status without needing an organization |

The stored URL is checked again by `remoteMcpUrlProblem` before every fetch, so a row edited by hand in D1 is refused like one registered (`mcp.remote.refused`), and none of the caller's headers go upstream. | A server redirecting the gateway's credentialed request to a third party; a hand-edited row pointing the gateway at a host the registration rules refuse. | `remote/client.ts` (RemoteMcpClient: redirect manual) and `remote-mcp/index.ts` (refusal), #627; `packages/sdk/src/internal/remoteMcp.ts:230` (remoteMcpUrlProblem) | `packages/toolbus/src/remote/client.test.ts :: sends the credential only to the registered URL and follows no redirect`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/remote-mcp/remote-mcp.sqlite.test.ts :: forwards a call with the credential to the registered URL only, and never to a redirect` **Pinned (PR 2, #627).** |

Only the operator sets it (`everyapp secret set`, or `mcp add`'s hidden prompt or `--from-env`), never the gateway's UI or a tool; the member's own OAuth or personal token never goes upstream. | The credential reaching an AI client, a member, the audit trail or the logs; a server echoing the credential in its error body back to the model. | `remote/client.ts` (sanitizedHead, RemoteMcpError) and `remote-mcp/index.ts` (upstreamFailure, logUpstreamFailure, callTool's relay), #627; `packages/sdk/src/internal/remoteMcp.ts:551` (remoteMcpAuthHeaders) | `packages/toolbus/src/remote/client.test.ts :: never carries the credential or the upstream body in an error`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/remote-mcp/remote-mcp.sqlite.test.ts :: never puts the credential in a result, an audit row, a usage row or a log line` **Pinned (PR 2, #627).** |

Not this boundary's job: Who may deploy an app and so write its manifest's providers/capabilities and the installation's email senders (the deploy/CLI boundary, ADR-0003); listing a capability is the grant.; The provenance of the binding's upload-time props (ctx.props) and the Workers for Platforms dispatch namespace; this boundary trusts them once parsed.; Minting and verification internals of the identity JWT and the perimeter's stripping of inbound x-everyapp-* headers from browsers (identity/perimeter boundaries); this boundary only verifies aud/iss and reads orgId/chan/scopes.; The trust placed in shared first-party Workers' x-everyapp-org for background work: accepted because the operator deploys that code.; What an app does with the user tokens an OAuth token endpoint returns: the app holds them and calls the network's API directly; the gateway never mediates API calls.; App-composed mail content: recipients and HTML/text are the app's to choose; the gateway validates structure and headers, not content, and Reply-To is free.; Operator choices such as granting an app '*@<the gateway's own sender domain>' on self-hosted, or opening AI_PROXY_EXTRA_ROUTES, which are documented as the operator's risk.; Provider-side behaviour and shared quotas (Meta app-review tiers, TikTok user caps, Workers AI account limits, Resend daily quota): one key/client per gateway means these are shared by design.; The local-dev direct-to-provider fallback (EVERYAPP_DEV with developer keys in .dev.vars): not the gateway.; Egress policy for dispatched Workers (the Outbound Worker's reserved_host/reserved_endpoint denies), which keeps third-party apps from bypassing these proxies; a separate boundary.; Inbound mail (Email Routing rules to an app's email() handler): outside the perimeter, documented as untrusted input..

With #411 merged (2026-10-24), five more invariants hold here: `oauth.scopes-declared-or-no-token`, `oauth.granted-scopes-within-sets`, `oauth.unverifiable-answer-refused`, `oauth.request-decided-on-body` and `oauth.scope-logs-names-only`; docs/threat-model.md B4 has them with their code pointers (commit 40159f82) and tests.

## B5. AI client → /mcp: the OAuth server, personal tokens and the ToolBus

51 invariants. This boundary decides who may list and call an app's agent tools through the gateway's one MCP connector (`${GATEWAY_URL}/mcp`) or, where an app's manifest opts in with `mcp: { by: "gateway", why }`, that app host's `/mcp`, and as whom. It protects the ability to act as a member inside every app they can use (tool calls mint a per-call identity JWT the SDK trusts), the OAuth material in OAUTH_KV (grants, codes, access and refresh tokens, consent transactions, registered clients), the `mcp_connections` rows that every token is re-checked against, personal access token hashes, and the `mcp_tool_calls` audit trail. The authorization server is @cloudflare/workers-oauth-provider 1.2.3 wrapped so that every grant is bound to one resource, PKCE S256 is mandatory, platform-domain client ids and redirect URIs are refused before any fetch, consent is single-use and browser-bound, and every `/mcp` request and refresh re-reads the connection, membership, ban and organization status from D1. Anyone on the internet may register a client, so registration is capped per isolate, per address and per gateway per day, and bodies on every OAuth route are read under a cap. The remaining structural weakness is the library's refresh rotation (a replayed refresh token is never detected) and personal access tokens that default to `*` for 90 days with no UI to narrow them. Since 2026-10-25 a second in-process app kind is served here, remote MCP apps (any MCP server registered by URL, its tools listed from the server and each call forwarded to it): the four `remote-mcp.*` rows hold its risk mapping, its per-call identity, who may register it, and the operator's per-tool off.

| switches.kill-and-dcr | With MCP_OAUTH_ENABLED off (false/0/off/no, or any unrecognized value, which fails closed with a warning) every `/oauth/*` path and OAuth discovery document answers 404, the gateway's `/mcp` accepts personal access tokens only, and its 401 names no authorization server; with MCP_DCR_ENABLED off the metadata names no `registration_endpoint`, `/oauth/register` answers 404, and clients registered before keep working. | An operator being unable to shut the OAuth surface or anonymous registration quickly; a typo in the switch leaving it on. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/oauth-flag.ts:25-41` (switchedOn fails closed), :43-54; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/routes.ts:102-121` (serveMcpWithTokensOnly), :208-218 (oauth_disabled 404), :227-230 (registration_disabled 404); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.ts:136-138` (no clientRegistrationEndpoint when DCR off) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: takes personal tokens only and names no authorization server; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: is off with MCP_DCR_ENABLED=false; clients registered before keep working; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/oauth-flag.test.ts` :: is on unless the switch says off |
| issuer.https-or-loopback-only | The OAuth server is built only when GATEWAY_URL is https, or http on exactly `localhost` / `127.x.x.x`; otherwise OAuth stays off and `/mcp` takes personal tokens only. MCP_ACCESS_TOKEN_TTL shortens access tokens (never below 60 s) only on an http loopback issuer and is a dev-only setting the CLI's `secret set` refuses. | Issuing OAuth tokens over plaintext http on a real host; a production operator shortening or lengthening token lifetimes by accident. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.ts:68-82` (canonicalIssuer), :89-98 (accessTokenTtl), :206-239 (getMcpAuth); `packages/sdk/src/internal/gatewaySettings.ts:142` (DEV_ONLY_GATEWAY_SETTINGS) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.test.ts` :: issues from GATEWAY_URL's origin, canonicalized; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.test.ts` :: accepts http only on exactly localhost or 127.0.0.1; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.test.ts` :: shortens access tokens for local testing only |
| mcp.browser-origin-rule | A request to the gateway's `/mcp` carrying an `Origin` is served only when that origin is the gateway's own or is listed (exactly, lowercased, http/https only) in MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS (empty by default); on the Cloudflare Access path this check runs before the assertion is verified or anything is read for the person; cross-origin CORS answers never allow credentials. | A page on another site driving tool calls with an ambient credential (an Access cookie behind Managed OAuth) or reading responses cross-origin. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/gateway-mcp.ts:85-97` (allowedOrigins), :106-111 (browserOriginAllowed), :399 (allowedOrigins on the view); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/routes.ts:182-191` (Access path checks Origin first); `packages/toolbus/src/http.ts:709-735` (refuseUntrusted), :737-755 (originAllowed), :758-766 (corsHeaders, no credentials) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: serves only the gateway's origin and MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a cross-site browser's request before looking at who it is; `packages/toolbus/src/http.test.ts` :: lets a browser call only from the app's own origin |
| access.assertion-header-only | On the gateway's `/mcp` a Cloudflare Access identity is taken only from a `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` header that verifies for the gateway's own application AUD (never from the `CF_Authorization` cookie, never the apps' AUD, never a service token); an assertion that doesn't verify is ignored and the bearer is checked as usual; the person must already have an `accounts` row linked by the outer ring (a user with merely the same email is never a fallback), be active, and resolve to one organization. | A cross-site POST riding the Access cookie on a bypassed `/mcp`; a forged or foreign-application assertion; an attacker who controls an email address an existing user registered taking over that account through Access. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/routes.ts:174-202` (serveMcpThroughAccess); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-caller.ts:56-73` (findLinkedUser), :80-105 (resolveOrganizationId), :111-145 (resolveAccessCaller) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: never reads the CF_Authorization cookie; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: ignores an assertion that doesn't verify, and then takes a personal token as ever; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: ignores an assertion of the apps' Access application |
| pat.format-and-hashing | A personal access token is `epat_` followed by 32 random bytes as hex; only HMAC-SHA256(BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, `every-app-gateway:user-pat:v1:<token>`) is stored, with a 9-character display prefix; verification requires the prefix, a hash match, no `revoked_at` and `expires_at` in the future. | Recovering tokens from a leaked D1 database; forging a token without the secret; a revoked or expired token being accepted. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.ts:18-35` (format), :133 (hash), :164-170 (verify); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/user-pat-hash.ts:7-25`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/UserPatRepository.ts:43-74` (findActiveByTokenHash) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: creates and verifies a PAT roundtrip; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: returns null for expired tokens; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: returns null for revoked tokens |
| pat.scopes-decide-mcp-access | On either `/mcp`, a personal token with `*` or `mcp:write` is read & write, with `mcp:read` read only, and with any other scope set (API scopes, `deploy`) is refused `403 token_scope`; `provider:` scopes are never valid; a token bound to an app sees that app alone; the token's user must still be a live, non-banned member of the token's organization, which must be active. | An API-only or deploy token being used to drive tools; a read-only token calling write tools; a token outliving its holder's membership. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/validate.ts:95-100` (patAccess), :111-151 (resolvePatCaller); `packages/toolbus/src/principal.ts:26-67` (principalFromSession); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.ts:168-177` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: reads only with mcp:read, sees only its app, and refuses non-MCP tokens; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: serves every app a full token reaches, and audits it as the token; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: ends a held subscriptions/listen stream once the token is revoked |
| pat.deploy-key-opens-nothing | A deploy key (a token whose only scope is `deploy`, creatable only where third-party deploys are on) opens no app on any app host (the authenticator returns null → 401) and is refused `token_scope` on the gateway's `/mcp`. | A CI credential leaked from a pipeline being used to read or act in the owner's apps or tools. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:120-122`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/validate.ts:95-100`, :130-131; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.ts:68-83` | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: opens no app with a deploy key, an owner's included; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: refuses a deploy key with any other scope; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: makes no deploy key where third-party deploys are off |
| pat.mcp-only-tokens-stay-at-mcp | A personal token whose scopes are all `mcp:` scopes reaches an app's `/mcp` and nothing else, and only on a host whose manifest has `mcp`: every other app route, public ones included, answers `403 token_scope`, and so does `/mcp` itself on a host without `mcp` or where a stored public route covers it on a `by: "gateway"` host (nothing the Gateway vouches for serves MCP there, and the app would otherwise receive the token's identity on a route it doesn't scope-check); tokens holding `*` or any non-MCP scope keep working on every route. | An `mcp:read` token being used on an app's own routes, which apps don't scope-check, to read or write beyond read-only tool access. | `packages/perimeter/src/session.ts:32-37` (isMcpOnlyCredential); `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts` (the `mcpServedHere` check in handleGatewayRequest) | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: refuse every app route but /mcp with 403 token_scope; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: open nothing on a host whose manifest serves no MCP, /mcp included; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: open nothing at a /mcp a public route covers, even where the manifest opts in; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: let them through at /mcp, to the ToolBus or to the app's own server; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: leave full-access and API-scoped tokens on every route |
| pat.bound-to-org-and-app | A personal token acts only in its own organization (never under another membership of the user), only on its bound app when it has one (also on shared apps), and fails when its user is no longer a member, is banned, or the organization is suspended. | A token for one app being used on a sibling app; a token minted in one organization reaching another the user also belongs to; a departed or deactivated member's token still working. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:55-76` (resolveLiveOrgRole), :79-92 (boundToAnotherApp), :112-122, :174-195; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/validate.ts:120-128`, :149 | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a token bound to another app; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: denies an app-scoped PAT for a different app in the same org; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.test.ts` :: keeps a token to its own organization's apps |
| pat.lifetime-and-ownership | A personal token expires: 90 days by default, 30 for a deploy key, and through the server function between 1 day and 1 year; tokens are created, listed and revoked only by the signed-in member for themselves (by user id), never for another user. | Tokens that never expire; one member listing or revoking another's tokens. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.ts:21-23`, :88-109 (parseExpiresAt), :172-184 (revoke by userId), :186-196; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/schemas/user-token.ts:15-27` (1 day to 1 year); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/serverFunctions/userTokens.ts:10-35` (organizationMemberMiddleware, context.user.id) | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: makes a deploy key last 30 days unless told otherwise; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/services/UserPatService.test.ts` :: returns null for expired tokens |
| apphost.mcp-session-or-pat-with-csrf | An app host's `/mcp` serves a browser session only from the app's own origin (a cookie POST needs `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` or, without it, an `Origin` on the app's host; a sibling app's page gets `403 csrf_denied`) or a personal token; only a POST makes the gateway sync a catalog from the app; an app whose manifest keeps `mcp: { by: "app" }` (or a stored one that declares `/mcp` public) is proxied to instead, with the identity; an app whose manifest has no `mcp` serves no MCP at all (its `/mcp` is proxied like any private route and the ToolBus is never asked); a cross-origin preflight is answered 401 by the perimeter. | A sibling app or another site driving tool calls with the shared session cookie; GETs with the cookie forcing the gateway to call the app; the ToolBus hijacking an app's own MCP server. | `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:350-374` (CSRF), :421-429 (interceptMcp conditions); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.ts:90-115` (POST-only sync), :141-177 | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: runs a tool for the app's own page and audits it as the session; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a sibling app's page on the same site; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: doesn't sync for a GET that carries the cookie |
| tools.policy-destructive-hidden-write-gated | Destructive tools are absent from `tools/list` and a call answers `Unknown tool` for every caller; write tools (and tools without a declared risk) are hidden from and refused for read-only callers. | An AI app deleting data through a tool the app marked destructive, or a read-only connection writing; a hidden tool's existence leaking. | `packages/toolbus/src/policy.ts:25-39` (evaluateToolPolicy); `packages/toolbus/src/server.ts:115-127` (listTools); `packages/toolbus/src/execute.ts:373-376`, :267-276 | `packages/toolbus/src/policy.test.ts` :: hides destructive tools from everyone and write tools from read-only callers; `packages/toolbus/src/policy.test.ts` :: treats an unannotated tool as write and lets the org block anything; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: hides destructive tools and blocks calling them |
| tools.per-call-identity | Each tool call reaches exactly one app over its binding with a fresh identity minted for that call: `chan: "mcp"`, explicit `mcp:read`/`mcp:write` scopes (never `*`, never `mcp:destructive`), `act.sub` naming the credential (`oauth:<connection>`, `pat:<id>`, `access:<email>`), a `tc` claim with the tool name and call id, and `aud` the app's Worker name; the app gets 30 s and at most 1 MiB of answer is relayed. | A tool call being replayed against another tool or app, an app receiving broader scopes than the connection holds, or a slow or oversized app answer tying up the gateway. | `packages/toolbus/src/execute.ts:87-91` (scopesForAccess), :98-109 (actorFor), :423-486 (one identity, one invocation), :65-67 (timeout, result cap); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/runtime.ts:86-96` (signing with JWT_PRIVATE_KEY, invokeApp over the binding) | `packages/toolbus/src/execute.test.ts` :: calls the app once with an identity minted for exactly this call; `packages/toolbus/src/execute.test.ts` :: mints read-only scopes for read access and the dev kid when asked; `packages/toolbus/src/execute.test.ts` :: acts as the OAuth connection and names its client in the audit |
| tools.request-shape-limits | JSON-RPC batches are refused on both protocol eras; bodies over 256 KiB are refused (chunked ones without reading them whole); arguments nested more than 64 levels are refused and still audited; arguments with more than 1,000 values and schemas with `$ref` or regexes the gateway can't loosen safely are left to the app's own validation, and the gateway never runs an app's regular expression; validators are cached per registry row and exact schema. | Fanning one request into many tool calls; memory or stack exhaustion on the shared gateway from deep or huge arguments; ReDoS through an app-written `pattern`; one organization's schema validating another's calls. | `packages/toolbus/src/http.ts:70` (256 KiB), :155-160 (legacy reject, maxSubscriptions 0), :288-294 (batch refusal); `packages/toolbus/src/execute.ts:69` (depth 64), :201-248 (checkArguments); `packages/toolbus/src/validation.ts:29` (1,000 values), :50-68 (per-row cache), :150-188 (gatewayCheckableSchema), :229-245 (measureJson) | `packages/toolbus/src/http.test.ts` :: refuses JSON-RPC batches, whatever their routing headers say; `packages/toolbus/src/http.test.ts` :: refuses bodies over 256 KB; `packages/toolbus/src/http.test.ts` :: refuses a chunked body over 256 KB before reading it whole |
| audit.one-row-per-call-no-arguments | Every tool call on either surface, including calls refused for their arguments and calls made through everyapp_call_read or everyapp_call_write, writes one `mcp.tool_call` log line and one `mcp_tool_calls` row holding the caller, credential, tool, decision, outcome, a keyed HMAC digest of the arguments (BETTER_AUTH_SECRET under the `every-app-gateway:mcp-args:v1` context), a byte count, the client's self-description from the MCP handshake (`client_info`, `<name> <version>` cut to 200 characters, never verified) and `via`, the wrapper the call came through, when it did (the row then names the inner tool and digests the inner arguments, so a wrapped call and the same direct call digest alike, and the wrapper writes no row of its own), never the arguments or the result; the hourly cron deletes rows older than MCP_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS (90 by default, at most 3,650), up to 20,000 a run. | Tool use going unrecorded, or recorded twice or under the wrapper's name; secrets passed as arguments leaking into logs or D1; the audit table growing without bound. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/runtime.ts:43-81` (auditToolCall), :103-106 (digestArguments); `packages/toolbus/src/digest.ts:14-39`; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/McpToolCallRepository.ts:38-63` (insert, with via), :100-199 (listForOrganization) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: registers as public, connects, calls tools, refreshes and revokes; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: prunes old tool calls, expires idle connections, and sweeps OAuth storage from a cursor; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/repositories/mcp-tool-call-list.sqlite.test.ts` :: keeps the wrapper a call came through (via), and lists it beside the tool |
| catalog.system-token-opens-only-the-catalog | A catalog sync is exactly one invocation of the app over its binding (in-process for a provider app) carrying the gateway's one-minute system token, which the SDK accepts only on `/__everyapp/tools/list` and never as an identity for a tool call; the internal tools paths answer 404 to every request from outside the gateway. | The catalog credential being replayed to run tools; an outside caller reaching the internal tools contract. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/catalog-sync.ts:148-160` (fetchAppCatalog with signing); `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.ts:295-299` (isReservedToolsPath → 404) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: snapshots the app's tools with one invocation and a system token; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/toolbus/app-host-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: never lets the system token call a tool, inside or outside |
| oauth.bodies-capped-and-rate-limited | The token endpoint body is capped at 64 KiB, a registration at 16 KiB and the consent decision at 64 KiB, each refused with 413 while being read (so a chunked upload can't exhaust the isolate) without spending any consent; POST /oauth/register and /oauth/token also pass the Workers rate limit on client address (60 per minute) while OAuth is on. | Memory exhaustion of the shared isolate through oversized or chunked OAuth bodies; brute-forcing codes or refresh tokens at the token endpoint at full speed. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/routes.ts:63`, :283-284; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/registration.ts:31`, :49-50; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/authorize.ts:46`, :184-187 | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses an oversized body without reading all of it; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/registration.test.ts` :: refuses a body over 16 KiB, without reading the rest of it |
| cimd.only-under-strictly-public-fetch | Client ID Metadata Documents are fetched only under the `global_fetch_strictly_public` compatibility flag (the library advertises and uses CIMD only with it), with a 10 s timeout, a size cap and a Cache API cache; so a metadata fetch never reaches an origin that isn't publicly routable. | SSRF from the authorization endpoint into private networks or the operator's own origins via an attacker-chosen `client_id` URL. | `apps/every-app-gateway/cloudflare.config.ts:179` (compatibilityFlags); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/config.ts:141` (clientIdMetadataDocumentEnabled); `apps/every-app-gateway/node_modules/@cloudflare/workers-oauth-provider/dist/oauth-provider.js:1072-1074`, :1466-1540 | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: advertises one resource, CIMD and public clients |
| mcp.gateway-paths-closed | On the gateway host only `/health` exactly (GET and HEAD, no session read; other methods 405), `/mcp` exactly, the two discovery documents, `/oauth/authorize` (GET only), `/oauth/token`, `/oauth/register` (when DCR is on), `/oauth/consent` and `/oauth/consent/decision` (POST only) are served; any other `/mcp/*`, `/oauth/*` or OAuth discovery path answers 404, and a failure answers each caller in its own shape (503 page, OAuth error or JSON-RPC error) rather than an uncaught exception. | Reaching library endpoints the gateway didn't mean to expose; error pages leaking stack traces; a `/mcp/<anything>` path confusing routing. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/health.ts` (serveHealth); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/routes.ts:127-148` (serveMcpAuthRoute), :204-244 (dispatch), :312-344 (unavailable); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/oauth-flag.ts:56-62` (isOauthPath) | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.test.ts` :: serves /health for uptime monitors: a bounded SELECT 1, no session, no version, never cached; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: describes /mcp at its path-aware metadata URL, with the exact resource; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: answers a failure with a page or an error its caller reads; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/gateway-mcp.e2e.sqlite.test.ts` :: takes personal tokens only and names no authorization server |

Not this boundary's job: Compromise of the operator's Cloudflare account, OAUTH_KV, D1 or the gateway's secrets: with BETTER_AUTH_SECRET an attacker can forge PAT hashes and with JWT_PRIVATE_KEY mint identities; those are the platform/operator boundary.; What an app does with a valid tool call: the SDK verifies the per-call identity and its scopes against the tool's declared risk, but a tool that under-declares its risk (a `read` tool that writes) is the app author's problem, not this boundary's.; A user approving consent for a client that is what it says it is but is malicious or compromised (a real Cursor install on a stolen laptop); the consent page can only show the client's name, verified domain and redirect host.; The AI client's own custody of its access and refresh tokens (claude.ai, ChatGPT, Cursor, Claude Code keychains) and of personal access tokens pasted into their configs.; Phishing or UI redress of the consent page beyond framing and caching (the arm-delay in ConsentForm.test.tsx is a mitigation, not a guarantee).; Prompt injection through tool results, app instructions or tool descriptions (instructions are clipped to 1 KiB per app and 8 KiB total, but their content is the app's).; Correctness of the Cloudflare Access configuration (which paths are bypassed, Managed OAuth settings, allowed callback URLs) and Better Auth session security; both are other boundaries that this one consumes.; Volumetric denial of service beyond the stated caps (body sizes, registration budgets, 60/min credential rate limit); Cloudflare's own protections are assumed.; Trustworthiness of the domain hosting a Client ID Metadata Document beyond the verified-domain rule (the page shows `claude.ai` as verified because access is sent there, not because Anthropic was vetted)..



## B6. Cloudflare Access ↔ the Gateway

37 invariants. Cloudflare Access is an optional, self-hosted-only way to prove who someone is; Better Auth stays the only session system and the gateway's own tables decide membership. The boundary protects three things: that a Better Auth session is only ever created for, and (behind the outer ring) only ever honoured for, the person Cloudflare Access cryptographically vouches for on that very request; that an Access identity reaches an existing account only through Better Auth's trusted-provider linking rules (verified local email, or while signed in), never by email coincidence; and that strangers, deactivated users, an unclaimed gateway and non-gateway AUDs never get a session or /mcp access, with nothing created for them. Every assertion and ID token is verified in full (RS256 by the team JWKS with a fail-closed cache, issuer, AUD, expiry, email, type app for assertions) before it is believed, and apps never see any cf-access-* header or the CF_Authorization cookie. What Access does not cover (personal tokens, OAuth tokens, deploy authority, the CLI) is deliberately out of its reach: offboarding is deactivation.

| access.jwt.rs256-only | An Access token verifies only when its header says alg RS256 with a non-empty string kid and the signature checks against that key; alg none, HS256 keyed with the public key, another key under a known kid, and any other algorithm are refused without a JWKS fetch. | Algorithm confusion and unsigned tokens forged by a client that knows the team's public key. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.ts:26` (ALGORITHM); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.ts:230-231` (verifyAccessJwt header check); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.ts:238-245` (jwtVerify algorithms: [RS256]) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.test.ts` :: refuses an unsigned token (alg: none); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.test.ts` :: refuses an HS256 token keyed with the public key (algorithm confusion); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.test.ts` :: refuses a token signed by another key under a known key id |
