| access.config.two-auds | Only assertions whose aud includes a CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_AUD tag may sign anyone in (maySignIn); a CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_APPS_AUD assertion only vouches for an existing session; a malformed APPS_AUD turns the whole outer ring off. | An app host's Access token (a separate application, with its own cookie) being replayed on the gateway as a login; with a single AUD every app host's Access cookie would also be a gateway login, safe only while it stays out of reach of scripts. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/config.ts:234-247` (readOuterRing signInAudiences, appAudiences); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/config.ts:258-264` (audiences union); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring.ts:46-53` (maySignIn) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/config.test.ts` :: lets only the gateway application's AUD sign people in; the apps' only vouches; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: signs people in only with an assertion for the gateway's own Access application; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring-perimeter.sqlite.test.ts` :: takes the assertion of the separate Access application on the apps' hosts |
| access.outer-ring.app-token-only | An outer-ring assertion counts only with claims.type === "app"; Access's organization token (type org) vouches for nobody. | The team domain's own session token being presented as an application assertion. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring.ts:45` (verifyOuterRingAssertion type check) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: signs people in only with an assertion for the gateway's own Access application; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/mcp-auth/access-mcp.sqlite.test.ts` :: ignores an assertion that doesn't verify, and then takes a personal token as ever |
| access.outer-ring.sign-in-only-on-gateway-host-get | The outer ring creates or swaps a session only during a GET /get-session whose Host equals GATEWAY_URL's host and that carries a maySignIn assertion; on any other host or method a session is only vouched for, never made. | Signing people in on hosts Access doesn't protect (workers.dev, app hosts) or from server-side lookups. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:69-82` (hostOf, requestHost); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:98-103` (outerRingOf: off without GATEWAY_URL); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:274-278` (after hook: maySignIn, GET, gateway host) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: only acts on the gateway's own host; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: counts a session only with Access's word for that person, wherever it is looked up |
| access.outer-ring.session-counts-only-when-vouched | With the outer ring configured, every auth.api.getSession (the perimeter on app hosts, TanStack server functions, the OAuth authorize and consent pages, host-session handoff) returns null unless the request carries a verified assertion (header, else CF_Authorization cookie) whose email equals the session user's email. | A browser whose Access session ended, or whose person was removed from the Access policy, continuing to use a still-valid Better Auth session cookie; a stolen session cookie used without the person's Access session. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:253-288` (after hook on /get-session, vouch-only branch 274-288); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/jwt.ts:273-287` (readAccessAssertion header then cookie); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:157` (authenticate calls auth.api.getSession) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: counts a session only with Access's word for that person, wherever it is looked up; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring-perimeter.sqlite.test.ts` :: doesn't let a session through without Access's word for that person; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring-perimeter.sqlite.test.ts` :: sends someone whose session isn't the Access user's back to sign in |
| access.outer-ring.better-auth-endpoints-vouched | On Better Auth's own HTTP endpoints other than /get-session and /sign-out, the session cookies are removed from the request context when no verified assertion for the session's person is present, so the endpoint answers as if unauthenticated (401). | Someone holding a stale or stolen session cookie calling /update-user, /list-accounts, /change-password and the like without Access's word for that person. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:206-249` (before hook, withoutCookies 85-95) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: lets Better Auth's own endpoints use only a session Access vouches for |
| access.outer-ring.sessions-made-only-for-vouched-person | Behind the outer ring, a user or session created over HTTP by any flow other than the outer ring's own (password sign-in, invite sign-up, OIDC callback) is refused unless the request's verified assertion names the same email: ACCESS_REQUIRED without one, ACCESS_EMAIL_MISMATCH for someone else. | Signing a browser in as a second account (the owner's, say) while Access says it belongs to someone else, which the next session check would otherwise swap or which an admin would otherwise use unvouched. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:147-169` (user.create before hook); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:172-199` (session.create before hook); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:351-373` (assertAccessVouchesFor) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: won't sign an account in whose email isn't the one Access asserts, and says so; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: makes no session for a request that didn't come through Access; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: behind the outer ring too, signs in only the person Access vouches for |
| access.outer-ring.someone-elses-session-replaced | On the gateway's own session check, a live session for a different email than the assertion's is deleted and the Access person's own session is made (or, if they may not sign in, the cookie is cleared and null returned); an expired own session is replaced by a new one. | A shared browser keeping the previous person's gateway session once Access says someone else is at the keyboard. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:290-325` (after hook sign-in branch) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: swaps a session that belongs to someone else for the Access user's own; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: ends someone else's session even when the Access user may not sign in; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: signs the Access user in again when their own session has expired |
| access.outer-ring.unverifiable-assertion-is-inert | An assertion that doesn't verify (forged, wrong AUD, garbage) signs nobody in and honours no session, but also ends no session: the next request with a valid assertion for the session's person works. | A forged header on a bypassed path minting a session, or being used to log the real person out (denial of service on sessions). | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring.ts:31-45` (verifyOuterRingAssertion returns null); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:274-288` (no identity: return null without deleting) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: signs nobody in with an assertion it can't verify, and honours no session with one |

| access.perimeter.strips-cf-access-headers-and-cookie | Before proxying to an app the perimeter removes the cookie header (so CF_Authorization) and every header starting with cf-access- (assertion, authenticated-user-email, service-token id and secret); apps see only the gateway's identity JWT. | An app trusting a forged Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email on a host Access doesn't cover, or an app logging or leaking the Access assertion or cookie. | `packages/perimeter/src/headers.ts:27` (CF_ACCESS_HEADER_PREFIX); `packages/perimeter/src/headers.ts:44-59` (stripInboundHeaders) | `packages/perimeter/src/headers.test.ts` :: removes Cloudflare Access's assertion, email and service-token headers; `packages/perimeter/src/gateway.test.ts` :: (b) strips credentials and spoofed trust headers on a private route; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/outer-ring-perimeter.sqlite.test.ts` :: serves the app to the person Access vouches for, without Access's headers or cookie |

| access.membership.members-invitees-autojoin-only | An Access identity gets a session only as an existing member, a person with a live pending invitation for that exact lowercased email (claimed atomically, invited role, default apps), an email whose exact domain is in CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_AUTO_JOIN_DOMAINS (member role, default apps, the first owner's organization), or through the outer ring only with CLOUDFLARE_ACCESS_OUTER_RING_AUTO_JOIN=everyone; everyone else is refused with no user, account or membership created. | Anyone Access admits (or anyone at all, with OIDC) becoming a member without an invitation; a subdomain or look-alike domain (eng.acme.com, notacme.com) joining; two racing sign-ins both spending one invitation; everyone auto-joining through the SSO button when only the outer ring was meant to be the membership list. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/membership.ts:95-123` (pendingInvitationsFor, autoJoinRoute); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/membership.ts:145-155` (assertAccessUserMayBeCreated); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/membership.ts:163-200` (acceptPendingInvitations claims status first) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: makes an invited person a member, with the invited role and the default apps; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: lets people from an auto-join domain in as members, with the apps available to everyone; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: lets everyone the outer ring admits join when the operator says so |

| access.membership.verified-email-to-join | Only a user whose emailVerified is true is made a member through Access (Access-created users are written with emailVerified true). | A password account registered with someone else's address being joined to an organization by that person's Access sign-in. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/membership.ts:225` (ensureAccessMembership); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:168` (user.create hook sets emailVerified true) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: never lets an account whose email isn't verified join |
| access.linking.verified-local-email-or-signed-in | An Access identity is linked to an existing account with the same email only when that account's own email is verified (Better Auth trusted-provider linking) or while the browser is signed in to it as the same email (outer ring: a password sign-in through Access; OIDC: /link-social), and never to an account of a different email. | Account takeover by email coincidence: someone who registered a password account with another person's address getting that person's Access sign-in attached, or an attacker linking a victim's identity to the attacker's account. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/config.ts:355-364` (accountLinking trustedProviders: [cloudflare-access]); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:290-295` (same-email session links); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/plugin.ts:423-446` (linkSignedInAccount) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: asks an account whose email isn't verified to sign in once with its password, then links it; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: links an existing account only when its email is verified, else after a password sign-in; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: won't link a different email to a signed-in account |

| access.oidc.state-pkce-client-secret | OIDC sign-in uses Better Auth's browser-bound state cookie and PKCE S256 and redeems the code at the token endpoint with the client secret; a callback in a browser that didn't start the sign-in fails state_mismatch and a code planted from another sign-in fails invalid_code. | Login CSRF (finishing an attacker's sign-in in the victim's browser) and code injection. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/oidc-provider.ts:50-63` (createAuthorizationURL with state, codeVerifier, fixed scopes); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/oidc-provider.ts:64-78` (validateAuthorizationCode with clientSecret) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: sends the browser to Access with state and PKCE (S256), and signs an invited person in; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a callback in a browser that didn't start the sign-in (state); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/auth/cloudflare-access/access-sign-in.sqlite.test.ts` :: can't be handed a code issued for another sign-in (PKCE) |

The gateway's side: `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server.test.ts` :: answers a JSON 404, never the control plane, on every spelling of a bypassed prefix it doesn't serve |

Nothing in the CLI takes a provider off an application. | A provider that asserts an address nobody verified (a Cloudflare account registered under a member's address, a self-chosen OIDC email) passing the policy as that member, which the gateway would then link to the member's account (`accountLinking.trustedProviders` names the Access provider). | `packages/cli/src/lib/access/plan.ts` (allowedSignInProviders, allowedIdpsFor, desiredSignIn, signInInSync); `packages/cli/src/commands/access/index.ts` (turnAccessOn step 2, reconcileApplication, warnIfOpenToAnyCloudflareAccount) | `packages/cli/src/lib/access/plan.test.ts` :: allows only the PIN and a Cloudflare provider restricted to the account's members; the team's others are the operator's; `packages/cli/src/commands/access/index.test.ts` :: warns about a Cloudflare provider open to any Cloudflare account, on and show alike; `packages/cli/src/commands/access/index.test.ts` :: adds Sign in with Cloudflare to applications made before it, taking nothing off them |

Not this boundary's job: Cloudflare Access's own correctness: its identity providers, MFA and device posture, policy evaluation, session durations, the binding cookie, key rotation, and the operator's Zero Trust configuration (applications, bypass lists, Managed OAuth settings); the gateway only verifies what Access signs.; Which identity providers verify email addresses: the gateway trusts the email in any Access-signed token that doesn't say email_verified: false (recorded as a gap above, but the choice of providers is the operator's).; Bearer credentials: personal access tokens and the gateway's own OAuth access tokens never pass through Access (they are checked by the perimeter and the MCP resource server); removing someone from the Access policy is not offboarding, deactivation is.; Deploy authority: the CLI deploys with the operator's Cloudflare login and writes D1 directly (ADR-0003); Access neither guards nor is bypassed for it.; The hosted gateway: no Access sign-in at all, settings ignored.; Apps' own public routes bypassed at Access, and anything an app does with the identity JWT once it has it.; Email address recycling at the identity provider (noted as an info gap; the gateway keys accounts by email by design).; Session fixation and cookie tossing across the shared cookie domain, CSRF on cookie-carrying control-plane POSTs, and the perimeter's Origin rules (the perimeter and control-plane boundaries), except where the Access path adds its own Origin-first rule on /mcp.; Availability: with the team JWKS unreachable nobody signs in by Access and no outer-ring session is honoured; passwords still work unless turned off (fail-closed is the chosen trade-off)..



## B7. Operator → Cloudflare: deploy authority on self-host

38 invariants. On a self-hosted gateway, deploy authority is the operator's Cloudflare login (or CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN): the CLI writes the gateway's D1 (apps, user_app_access, organization_keys, the install row, the deploy lease, the resource ledger) with `cf d1 query` and uploads Workers with `cf deploy --prebuilt`. This boundary protects (1) the Cloudflare account from an app's cloudflare.config.ts: a built Worker is rewritten from tenant-neutral placeholders and then checked by packages/cli/src/lib/app/policy.ts so it is private (no workers.dev, preview URL, domain or route), carries the install's identity settings, binds only the gateway's AppGateway, its own helpers and the resources this deploy provisioned, and never moves or deletes Durable Object data without the flag; (2) the registry from name takeover and privilege creep: @every-app/perimeter/registry assigns `every-<orgKey>-<appId>`, refuses reserved ids and names held by another row (tombstones included), grants a new app only to the deployer, owners and admins, records the actor, and the gateway re-validates every row it reads and fails closed on unknown status/tier/scope; (3) secrets: values never on argv, reserved names refused, every change under a lease and only over a version everyapp recorded; (4) releases: a tarball is integrity-checked (SHA-256, kind, top-level entries) and held to the same install+policy as a local build, and a repository URL is never built from. The gateway's public Worker has no `/api/v1` deploy API on self-host, but it does serve a browser install route (`POST /api/admin/apps/install`) for any organization owner/admin once `CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN` is stored; that path is untested on self-host (docs/security-model.md "Operator Authority" records it).

| hyperdrive.own-origin-only | A Hyperdrive config is created from, or adopted only when it already connects to, the origin in the app's `.everyapp/secrets.env` (checked before anything is created; caching off by default), is recorded in the ledger, and the policy accepts only the provisioned config ids; the dispatch tier refuses Hyperdrive and R2 entirely. | Binding a config that holds another database's credentials, or a third-party app getting one of the account's 25 configs. | `packages/cli/src/lib/app/resources.ts:207-295` (provisionHyperdrive); `packages/cli/src/lib/app/policy.ts:238-242`; `packages/perimeter/src/manifest/manifest.ts:770-795` (manifestTierProblems) | `packages/cli/src/commands/deploy/deployApp.hyperdrive.test.ts` :: adopts a config of its name that connects to the database in the secrets file; `packages/cli/src/commands/deploy/deployApp.hyperdrive.test.ts` :: refuses a config of its name that connects elsewhere, before anything is uploaded; `packages/cli/src/commands/deploy/deployApp.hyperdrive.test.ts` :: creates no Hyperdrive for an app on the dispatch tier, which a CLI deploy refuses outright |

Not this boundary's job: Runtime perimeter checks on the app host (session termination, host routing, identity JWT minting and the SDK's verification) — the Perimeter boundary; this boundary only guarantees the Worker is built with the right keys/audience/issuer and the row is well-formed.; The hosted deploy API (`/api/v1/*`, deploy keys, Workers for Platforms dispatch namespace, Outbound egress) and hosted multi-tenant isolation — the Hosted boundary; cited here only where self-host shares its code (browser install pipeline).; Compromise or misuse of the Cloudflare credential itself (the operator's login, CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN in CI, or CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN on the gateway): ADR-0003 accepts account-wide blast radius and revocation is Cloudflare's alone; everyapp never tries to stop a Cloudflare-authenticated actor.; Dashboard or API edits to a deployed Worker (enabling workers.dev, adding a route, deleting GATEWAY_URL, changing secrets): noticed only at the next everyapp run through cf's strict mode / assertReplaceable; apps are assumed to stay private, to call the SDK verifier, and to keep GATEWAY_URL set.; The app author's own code: that it verifies identities with the SDK, scopes its data per organization, and doesn't export identity material; an app can only hurt itself by misconfiguring its identity keys.; Trust in the release publisher and the deployer's machine: a release's SHA-256 proves integrity, not who built it (no signing); `.everyapp/secrets.env`, the Gateway build cache and cf's own login store are same-user local files; the manifest's `build` command and postinstall scripts run on the deployer's machine by design.; Social engineering of an admin through `/install?source=<url>`: the link only pre-fills the page; the admin must click Install (documented in the runbook).; `everyapp dev --host 0.0.0.0` exposing the gateway-lite with the public dev keypair: a documented local-development choice (TODO: "`dev --host` takes an address.").; Availability/abuse limits on the gateway's release fetches (GitHub, oss.new) beyond size and timeout caps; rate limiting of /api/admin/apps/install is the control-plane rate limiter's concern.; Deployer access requiring a verified email (password accounts are never verified without Access): a usability consequence recorded in TODO.md ("Deployer access needs a verified email."), not a security gap..



## B8. The hosted tier

40 invariants. This boundary keeps customer code (third-party apps forked or deployed by an organization) from touching anything but its own Worker, its own D1 and Durable Objects, and the gateway's metered proxies. Customer code enters only through the deploy pipeline (apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy): a deploy key or an owner/admin's browser install, a bundle the policy rewrites or refuses, an upload into a Workers for Platforms namespace whose bindings are read back and compared, and a registry row on tier `dispatch` named `every-<orgKey>-<appId>`. At run time the gateway dispatches only to that name with per-invocation limits and a default-deny Outbound Worker; the app answers on a separate registrable domain (THIRD_PARTY_DOMAIN) where the Better Auth cookie never goes and each host has its own MAC-signed session, handed off with a nonce-bound single-use 60-second code and re-checked live on every request. Shared first-party apps are the inverse: one service-binding Worker of the platform organization, opened by any live member of any active organization on the hosted gateway only. The whole hosted tier is behind a hand-set launch gate (THIRD_PARTY_DEPLOYS exactly `on` on a hosted gateway) and is unverified against a live namespace; what does reach a self-hosted gateway is the browser install (on by default once CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN, CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID and APP_BASE_DOMAIN are set), which shares the tar reader, release checks, bundle policy and read-back but uploads account Workers and patches the gateway's own bindings, and that path has no test.

| handoff.code-bound-single-use-60s | A handoff code is an HMAC-SHA256 token (key: HKDF of BETTER_AUTH_SECRET under 'everyapp host session v1') with typ 'code', expiring 60 s after issue, naming the gateway session, user, app row, host, nonce hash and return path; the exchange accepts it once (an insert into rate_limit_counters only one request wins), only on the host and for the app row it names, and only in a browser whose __Host-everyapp_n hashes to its nh; a tampered, replayed, late, wrong-host or wrong-browser code is a 400 page with no cookie, and a session token is never accepted as a code. | Login CSRF (an attacker sending a victim a code to sign them in to the attacker's account), replaying an intercepted code, using a code for app A on sibling app B, and forging codes without the secret. | `packages/perimeter/src/hostSession.ts:39` (HANDOFF_CODE_TTL_SECONDS = 60); `packages/perimeter/src/hostSession.ts:117-190` (importHostSessionKey, signHostToken, verifyHostToken); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/host-session.ts:239-249` (issueCode claims) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/third-party-hosts.sqlite.test.ts` :: uses a code once, in the browser that started the handoff, on its own host, within 60 seconds; `packages/perimeter/src/hostSession.test.ts` :: verifies what the key signed, until it expires; `packages/perimeter/src/hostSession.test.ts` :: refuses another key's token, a tampered one, the wrong type and junk |

| host-session.live-recheck-every-request | Every request on a third-party host verifies __Host-everyapp_s (signature, typ 'session', expiry, host, app row) and then re-reads, live, that the gateway session row still exists and hasn't expired, the user isn't banned, they are still a member of the app's organization, the organization is active and the app is installed for them; signing out of the gateway, losing the app or losing the membership ends the host session on the next request. | A host cookie outliving the gateway sign-out, a revoked membership or a withdrawn app grant; a banned user keeping app access through an old host session. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/hostSessionAuthenticator.ts:64-83` (liveGatewaySessionUser); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/hostSessionAuthenticator.ts:106-132`; `packages/perimeter/src/hostSession.ts:45` (HOST_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS only bounds re-handoff) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/third-party-hosts.sqlite.test.ts` :: ends the host session with the gateway session, the membership or the app |

| tar.refuses-everything-but-files-and-dirs | The tar reader refuses (TarError → RELEASE_INVALID) a header whose checksum doesn't match, a base-256 or non-octal number, a truncated archive, an absolute path, any segment that is `.`, `..`, empty or holds a backslash or control character, a hard or symbolic link, a device or any other type, a file with no name, a path listed twice, and more than maxFiles files. | Path traversal inside the unpacked map, link tricks, a header crafted to desynchronise the reader, zip-slip style overwrites of release.json or worker.config.json by a later duplicate entry. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/tar.ts:26-43` (octal, checksumMatches); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/tar.ts:68-80` (SEGMENT, entryPath); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/tar.ts:96-159` (readTar) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: refuses %s; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: refuses a file listed twice and a corrupt header; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: refuses an archive with a path outside it, or that isn't gzip |
| release.bounded-anonymous-download | The gateway downloads only `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/latest/download/everyapp-release.tar.gz` (owner and repo validated, `.`/`..` refused) following at most 5 https-only redirects with no credentials, or the exact oss.new URL `https://oss.new/apps/<64 hex>` with no redirects and a 15 s timeout; the body is capped at 100 MB compressed and again at 100 MB unpacked (gzip bomb), and a declared content-length over the cap is refused before reading. | SSRF to arbitrary hosts from an install form, a redirect off https, a decompression bomb exhausting the Worker, a private repository's release leaking through the gateway's credentials (it sends none). | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.ts:53-72` (parseRepoUrl); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.ts:259-317` (capped, gunzip); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.ts:328-386` (download) | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: refuses an oversize release, by its length or once unpacked; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: downloads the latest release, following GitHub's redirects to its tag; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: takes %s |
| upload.bounded-and-path-checked | A multipart deploy upload is refused over 64 MB (content-length and counted bytes) or 10,000 files, JSON parts over 1 MB, a stamp whose protocol is below the gateway's floor (426 PROTOCOL_TOO_OLD) or malformed, and any module, asset or migration path with an empty, `.`, `..`, backslash or control-character segment; assets must start with `/`. | Memory exhaustion of the Worker, an older CLI's bundle the policy can't read, path smuggling into the module/asset namespace. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/bundle.ts:17-19` (MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES, MAX_FILES, MAX_JSON_BYTES); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/bundle.ts:45-52` (SEGMENT, relativePath); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/bundle.ts:67-87` (parseStamp) | **partial.** `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/deploy-api.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a stamp below the protocol floor, before anything is registered; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/release.test.ts` :: makes the DeployBundle a multipart upload of the same Build Output makes |
| install.admin-live-gate-before-download | A browser install needs a Better Auth session of a user who isn't banned and is, live, an owner or admin of the chosen active organization; availability (the launch gate or the self-hosted deployer) and the deployer's settings are checked before anything is downloaded; the manifest and bundle are refused before the app is registered; deployed_by_actor is the admin's email and the job records repoUrl, tag, version and gitSha. | A member or a signed-out caller deploying code into the account; a suspended organization installing; the gateway downloading 100 MB for a request it would refuse anyway. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/install.ts:50-104` (authenticateSession, authenticateAdmin); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/install.ts:178-181` (caller, assertAvailable, deployerSettings before fetch); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/install.ts:192-219` | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/install-api.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses a member, a signed-out caller and a suspended organization; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/install-api.sqlite.test.ts` :: installs an app from its repository's latest release |
| install.self-hosted-default-on | On a self-hosted gateway the browser install is available whenever BROWSER_APP_INSTALLS isn't exactly 'off' and CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN, a 32-hex CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID and APP_BASE_DOMAIN are set; it then uploads an account Worker (not a namespace script) under a lease shared with the CLI, refusing a Worker that exists without the installer's claim and tags (WORKER_OWNED), one changed outside it (WORKER_CHANGED), an already-installed app (APP_ALREADY_INSTALLED), a hostname another Worker or DNS record owns (HOSTNAME_TAKEN) and a gateway binding used for something else (BINDING_OWNED), and patches the gateway's bindings with `inherit` plus the new service binding. | The browser deployer taking over a Worker, hostname or gateway binding the operator or CLI owns; two deployers writing the gateway's bindings at once. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/browser-install.ts:21-31` (selfHostedInstallEnabled); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/browser-install.ts:57-98` (withInstallLease); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/deploy/run.ts:236-239`, 277-303 | **unpinned.** |
| shared.platform-organization-only | A shared app (scope 'shared') registers only in the install (platform) organization (SHARED_APP_NOT_PLATFORM), its scope never changes (APP_SCOPE_MISMATCH, tombstones included), it is never on the dispatch tier, and the CLI registers, redeploys or deletes one only on a gateway whose install row says hosted and only for a Cloudflare login that is a platform owner or admin by verified email (an API token is the account's own authority). | A customer organization publishing an app every organization opens; a member login replacing first-party code; a third-party Worker becoming shared. | `packages/perimeter/src/registry/registration.ts:465-500` (planRegistration); `packages/cli/src/lib/gateway/registry.ts:410-427` (assertPlatformAdmin) | `packages/perimeter/src/registry/registration.test.ts` :: refuses a shared app from any organization but the platform's; `packages/perimeter/src/registry/registration.test.ts` :: never changes an app's scope, tombstones included; `packages/perimeter/src/registry/registration.test.ts` :: refuses a shared app on the dispatch tier |
| shared.secret-set-checks-platform-admin | `everyapp secret set\|unset` on a shared app require a platform owner or admin login, as deploy and delete do. | A non-admin Cloudflare login changing a shared (first-party, every-organization) Worker's secrets through the CLI's shared-app path. |  | **NOT ENFORCED.** |
| shared.opens-for-active-members-hosted-only | A shared row opens for any live member of any active organization with no user_app_access row, with the member's own (session) organization and role in the identity, only when platformPolicy.sharedApps (hosted); a suspended organization's members, cookies and tokens get 403 everywhere; a personal access token acts in its own organization only; on a self-hosted gateway a shared row opens for nobody; listings (launcher, AI-connection consent, gateway /mcp catalog, Admin → Apps read-only) follow the same policy. | A suspended organization's people keeping first-party app access; a shared app on an operator's install; a token acting for an organization it wasn't made in. | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/perimeter/betterAuthAuthenticator.ts:175-180` (hasAppAccess, scope shared); `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/platform-policy.ts:170`, 197 (sharedApps) | `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/shared-apps.sqlite.test.ts` :: opens a shared app for members of every organization, with their own organization in the identity; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/shared-apps.sqlite.test.ts` :: refuses everything to a suspended organization's members, cookies and tokens alike; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/shared-apps.sqlite.test.ts` :: keeps a personal access token to its own organization |
| shared.never-on-third-party-hosts | A shared row (and any service-binding row) is never served on a third-party host, and a dispatch row with scope 'shared' is served nowhere. | First-party code answering on the customer-code domain with per-host sessions, or customer code answering as a shared app. | `packages/perimeter/src/hostRoutes.ts:70-73` (hostAdmitsApp: third_party requires tier dispatch && scope org) | `packages/perimeter/src/hostRoutes.test.ts` :: serves dispatch apps of an organization on third-party hosts only; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/third-party-hosts.sqlite.test.ts` :: serves customer code on third-party hosts only, and first-party Workers nowhere else; `apps/every-app-gateway/src/server/third-party-hosts.sqlite.test.ts` :: answers 404 for unknown third-party hosts, and 503 for a row on the wrong class of host |

Not this boundary's job: What customer code does inside its own Worker, its own D1 and Durable Objects, and the routes it chooses to make public in its manifest (the perimeter's public-route and server-function rules are another boundary).; Cloudflare's isolation of Workers for Platforms scripts (CPU, memory, the namespace itself) and the correctness of its REST API answers: the read-back trusts GET /bindings and nothing re-checks later changes made in the dashboard.; Whoever can read the gateway's secrets (CF_DEPLOY_TOKEN, BETTER_AUTH_SECRET, JWT_PRIVATE_KEY) can deploy into the namespace, forge host sessions and identities (docs/security-model.md, "Hosted Deploy API").; Who publishes a GitHub or oss.new release: the sha256 proves the archive is whole, not who built it; an admin installs a repository they trust.; Egress of service-binding Workers (self-hosted apps, first-party apps, browser-installed apps): no Outbound Worker; the operator trusts what they deploy.; Metering (the wallet) for provider calls attributed to an organization: not built; the opt-in per-app monthly cap is B4's (`cap.per-organization-limits`); the hosted preview stays invite-only.; The identity JWT's minting and verification and the AppGateway's attribution rules beyond the upload-time props pin (separate boundaries).; Real-browser behaviour of `__Host-` cookies, SameSite=Lax on the 303 back to the app host and the CSP form-action on the handoff page (listed as live checks in docs/hosted-platform-build.md §7, git history at 5ef30882).; Sibling third-party apps' own cookies and same-site status before everyapp.dev is on the Public Suffix List (the perimeter strips Domain and its own cookie names; apps' own document.cookie is theirs).; Availability: a deploy that runs out of its request budget after upload, DEPLOY_ABANDONED after five minutes, and sweeping stale jobs (documented operational limits)..
